          Title: Job Expounded by Theodore Beza: Partly in Manner of a
                  Commentary, Partly in Manner of a Paraphrase; Faithfully
                  Translated out of Latine into English
     Creator(s): Theodore Beza (original author)
                 Translator not identified on this edition's own title page
                  or front matter
    Print Basis: Cambridge: Printed by John Legatt, 1589
         Rights: Public Domain
       Language: eng
   Rights Basis: Published 1589 - more than four centuries before this
                  session (2026), unambiguously public domain by any
                  standard (predates the concept of copyright entirely).
                  archive.org's own automated copyright-status classifier
                  has not tagged this item either way ("possible-copyright-
                  status" absent); the PD determination here rests directly
                  on the verified 1589 imprint date, independent of that
                  classifier.
Source Collection: Internet Archive (archive.org), identifier jobexpth00bzet
   Content note: Acquired for the Reformed Cities (Zurich/Geneva) world
                  build, closing Source Acquisition Manifest item G4 -
                  Theodore Beza's own direct voice, previously entirely
                  unvendored (Doc_02 §6's own named acquisition gap; not a
                  marginalized-voice/Article 20 case, a straightforward
                  acquisition gap in an otherwise-prominent figure). This
                  is a commentary/paraphrase on the Book of Job, opening
                  with Beza's own dedicatory epistle to Queen Elizabeth I -
                  a real, first-person Beza text in direct 1589 English
                  translation, not a modern retranslation. Does not itself
                  supply Beza's own letters or the Tabula praedestinationis
                  (1555), which remain unacquired - a partial, not
                  complete, closure of G4.

NOTE ON THIS TRANSCRIPTION: the source is a raw OCR (djvu) text layer from
a scanned physical volume, not a clean digital edition. Runs of 2+ spaces
between words (a systematic OCR-layout artifact of the scan, not a feature
of the printed text) have been collapsed to single spaces throughout; no
word, spelling, or punctuation has been altered. This is a late-16th-
century black-letter-adjacent typeface using the long s (visually close to
"f") throughout - the OCR consistently misreads long s as "f" (e.g.
"faluation" for "salvation," "moft" for "most"), a known, disclosed OCR
artifact of this specific print run, not corrected here per this corpus's
standing practice of vendoring raw, uncorrected OCR. The title page and a
small number of leaves at the very head OCR as near-total garbage (the
scanner reading a decorative/illustrated page, not text) - left as-is,
since it is not part of Beza's own text. The dedicatory epistle to
Elizabeth I and the commentary body itself were directly sampled at
multiple points this session and read cleanly once the long-s artifact is
accounted for.
